Title: From Grammar Deltas to Token Masks: Incremental Constrained Decoding under Runtime Grammar Evolution

  • Authors: Hantao Hua, Jiming Su, Yonggang Che, Feng Zhu
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.FL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32807
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32807
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.32807]] From Grammar Deltas to Token Masks: Incremental Constrained Decoding under Runtime Grammar Evolution(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32807)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Grammar-constrained decoding enables large language models to generate outputs that satisfy formal constraints, but in code and domain-specific language generation, the relevant constraints are not always fully available before decoding and may emerge progressively from new declarations, inferred type information, or runtime analysis feedback. Existing structured-decoding engines efficiently handle fixed, predeclared, or previously seen constraint structures; however, when a first-seen grammar update changes the parser state induced by an already committed prefix, the straightforward solution reconstructs the constraint state under the updated grammar, repeating parsing work already performed over the prefix. We introduce GramDelta, an incremental framework for constrained decoding with append-only runtime grammar updates. GramDelta maintains parse state over the committed prefix and propagates only changes induced by newly added grammar rules through historical dependencies, avoiding prefix replay. It then maps the updated parse state to exact next-token masks via compilation-free factorized projection over shared historical charts, avoiding per-update mask-cache recompilation. GramDelta therefore preserves the result of full reconstruction while making update cost sensitive to the actual impact of a grammar change. Experiments across four model configurations show that GramDelta adds negligible overhead without updates; under sustained runtime grammar evolution, it reduces median first-seen grammar-update latency by 10.7--11.5x and reduces the additional TPOT overhead over unconstrained decoding by 53.1--81.9% relative to the strongest reconstruction baseline. These results enable efficient constrained decoding when constraints progressively emerge and evolve with the generated prefix.

Title: EPRO: Robust Policy Optimization Through an Imperfect Execution-Policy Predictor

  • Authors: Yibo Wang, Changyi Yang, Yuanxin Liu, Zhaoran Wang

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: RL for LLMs often separates rollout generation from policy training. Although the two numerical engines share model parameters, they can induce different policies. This creates a challenge beyond correcting a known training-inference mismatch: during learner updates, the execution policy corresponding to the current parameters cannot be directly observed. We view this as optimizing an unobserved execution policy through an imperfect predictor of its evolution. This perspective raises two fundamental questions: how can we estimate the execution policy's response to learner updates, and how much should we trust that estimate when optimizing the actual execution policy? We introduce Execution-Policy Robust Optimization (EPRO) to address these challenges. Starting from the observed rollout-time execution policy, we use training-policy changes to construct a differentiable predictor of the current execution policy, whose score yields Transported Score Centering (TSC). Our robust-improvement analysis reveals how prediction error limits the magnitude of reliably justified updates. For practical implementation, we introduce Policy Shift as an observable proxy for prediction risk and use Shift-Aware Soft Clipping (SSC) to attenuate risky updates. Across four Qwen3 model settings, EPRO achieves the highest average held-out accuracy among the compared methods on seven mathematical reasoning benchmarks.

Title: Breaking the Joint Action Space Dependence in Distributionally Robust Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning

  • Authors: Yue Wang
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32812
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32812
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.32812]] Breaking the Joint Action Space Dependence in Distributionally Robust Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32812)
  • Keywords: robust, generative
  • Abstract: We study the sample complexity of learning robust coarse correlated equilibria (CCE) in finite-horizon, general-sum, distributionally robust Markov games with known rewards and a generative model of the nominal transitions. Each player evaluates a policy by its worst-case return over total-variation (TV) balls centered at the nominal transition distribution of every stage, state, and joint action; this is the standard $(s,a)$-rectangular uncertainty set. Existing generative-model guarantees for this model scale with the number $\prod_iA_i$ of joint actions, where $A_i$ is the number of actions of player~$i$. Since this number grows exponentially with the number of players, these guarantees suffer from the curse of multiagency. We design an algorithm whose sample complexity depends on the action sets only through the largest individual action-set size $A_*:=\max_iA_i$. The algorithm uses $\widetilde O(SA_*H^5D^4/\varepsilon^4)$ transition samples and, with probability at least $1-\delta$, returns a Markov policy that is an $\varepsilon$-robust CCE from every initial state. Here $S$ is the number of states, $H$ is the horizon, and $D:=\max_i\min\{H,1/\rho_i\}$, where $\rho_i$ is the TV radius of player~$i$. Two ingredients make this possible: a plug-in estimate of the robust backup whose mean error does not depend on the number of states, and a single batch of samples per round that serves as bandit feedback for all players at once. The result shows that the dependence on $\prod_iA_i$ can be avoided without modifying the $(s,a)$-rectangular uncertainty set.

Title: SpatialArena: Where Do Geometric Foundation Models Stand?

  • Authors: Yu Chen, Haosen Yang, Su Ee Tan, Zhensong Zhang, Jifei Song, Jiankang Deng
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32816
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32816

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: 3D perception is shifting from task-specific pipelines to geometric foundation models: monocular depth networks, feed-forward 3D/4D reconstruction models, and vision--language models that predict geometry. However, their progress cannot be measured reliably. Published numbers are not comparable, because every paper samples frames, sets resolutions, and selects test splits differently. They are not robust because the alignment required for every up-to-scale prediction can outweigh the gap between models: fitting depth in the wrong space nearly triples its error, and standard trajectory alignment understates the error of failing methods by up to $2.6\times$. And they rarely cover long recordings, dynamic scenes, or aerial views. We present SpatialArena, an open arena that scores geometric models with the same metrics and rules, with robust and deterministic alignment. SpatialArena spans five tasks, from monocular depth to SLAM. It covers 23 public datasets with 418,929 images across indoor, outdoor, aerial, object-centric, and dynamic scenes, together with Spatial Arena, our own captures with accurate poses and depth. Evaluating 52 models, we find that single-image depth is close to saturation on public benchmarks but not on large, uncontrolled scenes, and that progress now comes from consistency across frames and views, where feed-forward 3D/4D models match or surpass monocular specialists and overtake video depth models. The benchmark, toolbox, and leaderboard will be publicly released.

Title: PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation

  • Authors: Meilong Xu, Ziyi Zhao, Bhagyashree Puranik, Shayda Moezzi, Yixiao Zhang, Kushan Thakkar
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32821
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32821
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.32821]] PhysMend: Verification-Guided Program Refinement for Physics-Aware Video Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32821)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Video generative models synthesize realistic scenes, yet their interactions often violate physics. A ball dropped onto sand should displace grains and settle into a crater, whereas a generated video may show it passing through the surface or leaving the sand flat. Post-hoc methods typically score generated videos with a vision-language model (VLM), but such scores offer limited guidance on which object or parameter to modify, and each correction costs another synthesis. Simulation-based pipelines instead run fixed stages before synthesis, so simulation errors reach the video. To address these limitations, we present PhysMend, a training-free framework for verification-guided program refinement. PhysMend describes the target event as an editable physical program of grounded objects, events, and required relations, paired with tunable execution parameters. Our key insight is to verify and repair the event on its simulated rollout and on the depth video that conditions the generator (depth control) rather than on generated videos, enabling quantitative violation measurement, targeted editing, and fast re-verification before synthesis. A hybrid verifier evaluates semantic event consistency, physical plausibility, solver stability, and visibility in the depth control. VLM judgments are confined to event semantics and cannot override failed physical measurements, which limits the impact of VLM hallucinations. Each failed check localizes the program fields eligible for repair, and its measured violation scales a bounded update of their parameters. Only accepted depth control reaches the generator, which runs once per input. Experiments on the commonly used benchmarks Physics-IQ, PhyGenBench, and VBench-2.0 demonstrate substantial gains across different generator backbones; for example, PhysMend raises the overall Physics-IQ score of Wan-VACE from 27.33 to 39.40.

Title: Weight Scale Rebalancing: Transferable Training Recipes for Transformers

  • Authors: Yikun Hou, Naomi Graham, Alp Yurtsever
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32866
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32866
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.32866]] Weight Scale Rebalancing: Transferable Training Recipes for Transformers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32866)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Training Transformers requires careful hyperparameter tuning, which becomes increasingly costly for large models. We introduce UDV-Rebalance (UDVR), a reparameterization that inserts an auxiliary diagonal matrix $D$ between naturally paired weight matrices $(U,V)$ appearing throughout Transformer architectures. UDVR periodically rebalances the factors to maintain unit-norm columns in $U$ and unit-norm rows in $V$, with $D$ absorbing the corresponding scales. With rebalancing, a single hyperparameter setting of UDVR remains effective across model scales, architectures, and data distributions. This yields a tuning-free rule that adapts to each weight matrix using only its dimensions. We fit this rule once on ViT models trained on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100, and it remains valid when the activation function or the optimizer is changed. The same rule transfers to new image domains in DomainNet, to ViT, Swin, and MaxViT on ImageNet, and to language-model pretraining with up to 1.3B parameters. UDVR consistently outperforms Weight Normalization and $\sigma$Reparam and matches a vanilla baseline whose hyperparameters are swept on each target model.

Title: AVQ-Attention-2: Adaptive Vector-Quantized Attention with Exact Local Blocks and Linear-Cost Training

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.32989

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Attention costs $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$ in the number of tokens. Vector-quantized attention reduces this to $\mathcal{O}(NM)$ by clustering the $N$ keys, attending to the $M$ codewords instead, and its adaptive form, AVQ-attention, concentrates codebook resolution where a query's attention mass actually falls. We present AVQ-Attention-2, a better design that merges it with exact local attention and attends to the mean of each cluster's keys rather than to a codeword. We show three improvements: consistently better quality at comparable cost; training that now also costs $\mathcal{O}(NM)$ rather than $\mathcal{O}(N^2)$; and an approximation accurate enough that the layer can be dropped into a pretrained transformer without fine-tuning. Across a variety of sequence lengths the result is competitive not merely with other approximate attention but with exact dense attention, running faster than FlashAttention at little to no loss in quality.

  • Abstract: Speculative decoding accelerates large language model (LLM) generation using a lightweight draft. LEGOSpec decomposes draft models into Context, Backbone, Proposal, and Objective components that share a unified training infrastructure. Using LEGOSpec, we systematically vary components and evaluate selected combinations under matched training conditions to isolate their effects and interactions. (2) Proposal design must balance acceptance and latency. (3) The training objective should match token distributions locally and optimize acceptance length across the block. These findings guide workload-aware configurations and DFlux, a balanced general-purpose draft combining a convolution-equipped SWA--GQA hybrid backbone, Residual Corrector, and hierarchical supervision. Across Qwen and Gemma models, DFlux improves acceptance length over the strongest baseline by up to 13.7% on regular benchmarks and 34.7% on long-context workloads. To support efficient training, LEGOSpec integrates metadata-aware scheduling and asynchronous execution, increasing training throughput by up to 79% and accelerating offline supervision preparation by up to $3.11\times$ over SpecForge.

Title: Leto: Utility-Centric Agent Memory

  • Authors: Shuguang Chen, Salman Paracha
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33005
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33005
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33005]] Leto: Utility-Centric Agent Memory(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33005)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large language model (LLM) agents rely on external memory to retain and reuse information across interactions. Existing memory systems treat memory as a record of the past and evaluate it by fidelity to the conversation. However, the value of memory lies in whether it improves the agent's response. A memory system decides what to memorize and what to serve before that response exists, and fidelity checks neither decision against it. Faithful memory can therefore fail to improve the response. We instead view memory as a prediction problem and present \textbf{\emph{\leto{}}}, a memory system for LLM agents that focuses on the utility of memory to the future rather than on its fidelity to the past. Because future requests are unknown, \emph{\leto{}} preserves access to conversation details through memory indexing, allowing information to remain available through its sources. When a request arrives, memory replay draws on this index to anticipate and supply the context the agent needs beyond what the request explicitly states. The utility of the served context remains uncertain until the response is generated, motivating a utility loop that uses response outcomes to refine future memory selection. We evaluate our system against six memory systems on five benchmarks across three scenarios. In personalization, it outperforms the strongest compared memory system by 16.3 percentage points in accuracy on PrefEval and by 3.1 points in task completion on PERMA. For long-horizon tasks, it achieves the best overall performance on MemoryArena averaged over five environments with a gain that increases with the chain depth. In long-context understanding, it is on par with retrieval over the raw history while serving fewer context tokens.

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33050

  • Abstract: Answers from generative AI chatbots built on large language models (LLMs) now arrive at very different speeds: reasoning models deliberate before replying, while response caches can return a stored answer at once. Third, in 4,378,514 WildChat replies from everyday ChatGPT use, replies slowed by congestion made users less likely to continue. Delivery moved blind votes, in the direction opposite to the one the arena's designers expected, while waiting cost follow-up questions that a comparison between two answers cannot register when both are slow. The results bear on how AI services cache and stream answers, how they budget latency, and how preference leaderboards evaluate models.

Further analyses with artificially injected label noise reveals that clipping leads to more robust predictions, which stems from the implicit down weighting of hard-to-fit training examples.   • Authors: Yisrael Clark, Guy Katz
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33078
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33078
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33078]] FOL-Proof: Verifying LLM Text-to-Logic Translations via Deterministic Semantics(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33078)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly used to translate natural-language statements into first-order logic (FOL) to improve reasoning. However, LLMs are prone to errors, and so a semantically unfaithful FOL formula could corrupt downstream reasoning. We present FOL-Proof, a semantic firewall pipeline that validates LLM-generated FOL translations against independently derived linguistic interpretations. FOL-Proof operates by deterministically obtaining a pool of candidate translations via semantic analysis tools, such as the English Resource Grammar and Minimal Recursion Semantics; then applying an SMT solver to check each derived reading for logical equivalence to the LLM-generated formula. If the translation is faithful it is accepted; otherwise, it is rejected, or optionally repaired via repeated invocations to the LLM. The procedure is conservative: incomplete search, unsupported constructions, or inconclusive checks yield abstention rather than false rejection. We evaluated FOL-Proof on established benchmarks (ProofWriter, RuleTaker and PrOntoQA), and also on a new adversarial benchmark (LongFaithCheck). Our experiments show that FOL-Proof attains near perfect selective accuracy, while direct LLM judges and round-trip baselines show substantially worse performance.

Existing simulators largely rely on prescribed sensor dynamics and estimated brightness trajectories, whose assumptions limit the diversity and fidelity of plausible event streams. We introduce EventDiffusion, a hierarchical generative framework that directly learns the conditional distribution of native asynchronous event streams from real recordings. EventDiffusion decomposes generation into three probabilistic processes: Event Activity Diffusion (EAD) learns stochastic discrete event counts; Event Spatial Allocation (ESA) distributes these counts to individual pixels while preserving cardinality; and Event Timing Diffusion (ETD) learns continuous asynchronous timestamp distributions. We also introduce CEFS, an event-stream realism metric combining event activity and polarity with multiscale space-time structure and temporal fidelity. Across all datasets, EventDiffusion achieves double the accuracy of SOTA methods and demonstrates robustness across different cameras at any resolution.

Title: RenGen: Generating Images with as Many Tokens as the Scene Needs

  • Authors: Savya Khosla, Jay Mahajan, Akin Tuna Sakalli, Derek Hoiem, David Forsyth, Alex Schwing
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33109
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33109
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33109]] RenGen: Generating Images with as Many Tokens as the Scene Needs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33109)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: We present RenGen, a latent diffusion model that generates simple scenes from a few tokens and busy scenes from many, saving time and compute on simple scenes without losing realism. Most latent generators represent every image as a fixed grid of patch tokens, so a simple image costs as much to generate as a complex one. Prior methods that vary the token count through content-adaptive tokenization lose image quality as the count shrinks. RenGen instead trades scene complexity, rather than image quality, for fewer tokens. It does so by generating in the latent space of a pretrained region encoder, where each token represents one region of the image, and a decoder renders these region tokens back into pixels. Unlike patch tokens, region tokens cover areas of varying shape and position, so each token must capture both what its region contains and where it lies on the image canvas. We encode this spatial extent by adding positional embeddings to the encoder features before pooling, and we generate it by anchoring each token to a single randomly sampled point, from which the generator infers the region's shape, size, and content. At sampling time, the number of anchor points sets the trade-off between scene complexity and compute. On ImageNet $256{\times}256$, RenGen reaches a gFID of 1.34 with 88 tokens per image on average, the best among methods that vary their token count. It approaches the quality of RAEv2, a state-of-the-art patch-based model, at $2.5{\times}$ lower latency, advancing the Pareto frontier of generation quality versus compute. RenGen's gFID stays flat across scene complexity while latency varies by $2.4{\times}$ between simplest and busiest scenes, and its autoencoder reconstructs nearly as well as RAEv2 (rFID 0.32 vs. 0.30) with $2.9{\times}$ fewer tokens.

Title: Draft Calibration for Speculative Decoding: Accelerating LLM Serving by Matching Draft and Target Distributions

  • Authors: Yuxing Liu, Wei Zuo, Walid Krichene, Tong Zhang, Li Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33111
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33111
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33111]] Draft Calibration for Speculative Decoding: Accelerating LLM Serving by Matching Draft and Target Distributions(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33111)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Speculative decoding accelerates large language model (LLM) inference by employing a lightweight draft model to propose draft tokens and allowing the target model to verify the new tokens in parallel to improve inference-time compute utilization. The speedup relies on the acceptance probability of draft tokens in target verification, which is directly related to the gap $\|p-q\|_1$ between the target distribution $p$ and the draft distribution $q$. In this paper, we study draft calibration of speculative decoding to improve the speedup of speculative decoding, where we employ post-hoc methods to calibrate the draft distribution to mitigate the distribution gap. We observe that though current community open-source draft models may learn the top candidate token correctly, they are generally underconfident compared to the target model, suggesting a consistent distribution mismatch. To mitigate the effect of this mismatch on the acceptance rate of the draft models, we try various post-hoc calibration methods, including standard temperature scaling and token-wise calibration. Our results suggest that a standard temperature scaling applied to the draft distribution improves the acceptance length by up to approximately 10\% with almost no additional cost across different tasks. Throughput analysis further verifies that this improvement in serving efficiency. Analysis of more general calibration methods reveals further potential room for improvement.

Title: SAG-Mem-1.0 Technical Report

  • Authors: Yuchao Wu, XingCheng Liang, Xuanze Wang, Yini Huang, Yue Wu
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.IR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33124
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33124
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33124]] SAG-Mem-1.0 Technical Report(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33124)
  • Keywords: extraction
  • Abstract: Long-term conversational memory requires retrieving complementary facts scattered across dialogue sessions, including intermediate evidence with limited similarity to the original question. Existing approaches often rely on derived memory representations or persistent relational structures, introducing construction costs and potentially losing source details. We present SAG-Mem, a conversational memory framework that preserves complete dialogue messages in non-overlapping chunks and discovers complementary evidence through query-time lexical expansion. Memory construction requires no model inference, using a lexical index without LLM-based extraction, summarization, or semantic relation construction. At query time, BM25 retrieves initial candidates, an evidence evaluator identifies useful chunks, and lexical cues from selected evidence guide an additional retrieval round. Retrieved dialogue is further annotated with calendar interpretations of supported relative-time expressions, preserving the original wording and temporal granularity. We also introduce SAG-Mem-Lite, an alternative configuration that replaces evidence scoring with embedding-based candidate scoring, achieving lower operational cost than Full Context and all memory-based baselines across both benchmarks with low query latency. On LoCoMo and LongMemEval, SAG-Mem achieves overall scores of 83.08 and 80.20, respectively, the highest among the evaluated methods on both benchmarks. On LongMemEval, it exceeds the strongest compared baseline by 7.20 points. SAG-Mem-Lite achieves scores of 77.09 and 77.20, respectively. These results support dialogue preservation and evidence-guided retrieval expansion as an effective design for long-term conversational memory.

Title: One Take, Many Cameras: Audio-Grounded Shot Synthesis for Dialogue Re-Cinematography

  • Authors: Anthony Chen, Naomi Ken Korem, Matan Ben-Yosef, Tavi Halperin, Urska Jelercic, Ofir Bibi, Daniel Cohen-Or, Or Patashnik, Maneesh Agrawala
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33147
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33147
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33147]] One Take, Many Cameras: Audio-Grounded Shot Synthesis for Dialogue Re-Cinematography(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33147)
  • Keywords: robust, diffusion
  • Abstract: Creating a dialogue scene typically requires cutting between multiple views (e.g., close-ups, medium shots, and over-the-shoulder shots) of the actors. Yet capturing such coverage is costly and in many settings unavailable. We study dialogue re-cinematography: synthesizing additional shots from a single wide-angle dialogue video. Existing video generators can synthesize new shots, but rely on visual cues alone, so when the source video barely shows the lips, the generated lip motion fails to match the speech. Our key observation is that the speech audio is view-invariant: it constrains lip motion in any target view. Building on this insight, we adapt a pretrained audio-visual diffusion model to generate the new shot from a target frame that specifies the view, a source video that contains the performance, and the speech audio that drives lip motion. In multi-person scenes, however, pretrained audio-visual models drive every visible face with the same speech, causing speech leakage, where silent listeners mouth the speaker's words. We therefore introduce speaker-aware audio gating, a training-free module that lets the audio drive only the faces that speak in the target shot. On a cinematic paired-view benchmark spanning diverse viewpoints and framings, our method lowers perceptual error by 59%, landmark error by 56%, and speech leakage by 97% relative to the strongest baseline, Wan 3.0. Our method also remains robust on in-the-wild scenes, highlighting its potential for film post-production.

Title: PointX: Multi-View Consistent Intrinsics by Querying Surface Points

  • Authors: Miguel Farinha, Sam Sartor, Iliyan Georgiev, Giuseppe Vecchio, Ronald Clark, Michael Fischer
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33157
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.33157
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.33157]] PointX: Multi-View Consistent Intrinsics by Querying Surface Points(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33157)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: The appearance of a scene is determined by the albedo, roughness, metallicity and normal of every point on its surface, and by the light that reaches it, its irradiance. Recovering these intrinsics from images is ambiguous, and methods that predict them per pixel give the same surface point a different decomposition in every view. We introduce PointX, a simple feed-forward model that predicts intrinsics per 3D surface point instead. A multi-view transformer encodes a set of unposed images, and for any queried surface point, PointX pools the point's tokens across the views in which it appears and decodes the point's intrinsics once. Each point of a 3D reconstruction of the scene, such as a point cloud or a mesh, thus receives a single prediction, which is exactly consistent across views by construction and enables view-consistent relighting and editing. The same model also predicts per-view maps, from one image or many, by querying the point seen at every pixel. These maps agree across views up to the precision of the estimated geometry and correspondences. PointX is significantly more consistent than existing methods, while remaining competitive in accuracy from a single image and improving as views are added. Project page: this https URL
